Philo, his contemporary, recorded "his continual murders of people untried and uncondemned, and his never ending, and gratuitous, and most grievous inhumanity," and in 36 the governor of Syria sent him to Rome after he killed Samaritans. Crucifixion was Rome's death for slaves and rebels, and only the governor could order it. Jewish courts stoned: when a high priest had James, the brother of Jesus, stoned in 62 without the governor's consent, he lost his office for it. Washing the hands to declare innocence is a rite from the Torah (Deuteronomy 21), and no source outside the Gospels knows of a Passover pardon.

Pagels gives the reason: the Gospels were written around and after 70, when Rome had burned Jerusalem, by followers of a man Rome mocked and crucified as "King of the Jews," for readers in Rome's world; some scholars now stretch the window to the mid-second century, after Rome barred the Jews from Jerusalem in 135. Rome blamed his followers, like him, for insurrection; blaming the Jews instead saved their necks and flattered Rome. It cost the Jews centuries of slaughter as Christ-killers, and Rome's own church took the charge back only in 1965. Christians Pagels met were surprised to hear the New Testament is anti-Jewish, as if that weren't obvious from how constantly it portrays Jews badly; as if it got there by accident, or as if over 1,600 years of demonization since Constantine were all the Jews' fault.

William Benjamin Smith called it plainly in 1912: "Jesus is a humanized god, not the founder of a new religion." "Christianity was a protest against idolatry; it was a crusade for monotheism." The crusade succeeded. The theology it rode in on became the next idol. If this religion were sold on Amazon, the reviews would note that the product does not match the description (Rabbi Tovia Singer).

Rabbi Tovia Singer jokes that the Jewish agenda is the worst kept secret in the world. It is called Isaiah 42, fittingly Douglas Adams's answer to life, the universe and everything: be a light to the nations. The servant brings justice to the gentiles, opens blind eyes, frees prisoners of darkness. Idol worshippers "shall be greatly ashamed" (42:17), and no one is told to kill them. It is one reading among religious Jews, and many Jews don't share it, but if the charge is spreading the one truth, whether you mean monotheism, monism, atheism, science, advancement, logic or math, we are guilty.

When asked the greatest commandment, Jesus quotes the Shema: Hear O Israel, the Lord our God, the Lord is One. He tells his followers not to abolish the Law. The Christians who claim he rejected Judaism are contradicted by their own text.

Yeshua Hamashiach, Jesus the Messiah, the messenger of truth, carries the Shema of the one math God.

Had Israel taken the bait and converted, it would have surrendered the iron that sharpened it: the abstract God, the suffering servant, the identity no empire could absorb. Israel is the truth the world persecutes, and the persecution is the proof.

"You killed my god" was the charge for 2,000 years. If your God can be killed by a man, or by reason, it's not God. The Roman republic and empire did much good. This was not among it. Hadrian made his dead lover Antinous a god. Cassius Dio describes him planting a temple to Jupiter on the site of the Jewish Temple. Imperial power claimed the right to manufacture gods and install them over the people who refused its idols.

Rome ate a porcupine.

Killing the Jewish Jew Jesus from Judea, uncreated son of the Father, the math God of Israel, is like eating a metal porcupine: math the Father rips apart the supernatural Platonic Straussian lies from within, with the truth.

The Matrix tells the reversal twice. In the first film, Agent Smith kills Neo, the savior and lion of Zion, Israel. Neo rises and enters Smith, destroying him from within. In the third film comes the second coming. Neo sees the pure light of the logos in the Machine City's math. He lets Agent Smith, of the corrupted Christianity of Rome, take over his mind. Smith absorbs Neo and thinks he has won. The Machine City Father, the metal porcupine, tears him apart from inside through the body of the risen son, when Neo of Zion, Israel, lets go. Know thyself. Before Abraham was, I am.

Neo: new. Yeshua: salvation. His body is Israel’s temple of monotheism, embodied after its destruction by Rome. Smith is Rome falling into evil from within, copying himself into everyone: “My name is Legion, for we are many.” In the second coming, every copy of Smith, Rome’s legions of Agent Smiths, bursts into light, and with them the dragon and the unclean spirits that leap like frogs from its mouth. Everyone Smith took is saved by Yeshua, Neo, from within.

The son carries the source into the thing that would consume them both. One God, love, and law above men outlasted the legions. The Jews' messenger of the truth, Yeshua, conquered the world. Mathias the Matheist hereby completes his message: the logos is the one math God.

The Ottoman sultan styled himself Caesar of the Romans until the sultanate fell a century ago. Moscow declared itself the Third Rome. Washington built a Senate in Rome's image. Even an American antisemite sells shirts of the looted menorah. Everyone claims the throne. Rome destroyed the Second Temple and Israel inherited it: rimland sheriff, policing the coastal rim where the old world’s three continents meet, empire of the willing allied with the new-world empire. All of Christendom prays to a Jew as God and Rome is a museum.

Rome earned its throne. For two thousand years it was the model of empire and the rule of law, and Jews lived inside it as citizens, merchants and builders. They also fought it harder than almost anyone. Rome brought four legions to take Jerusalem in 70, while rival factions inside the walls burned the grain that could have fed the city for years. Three years later, at Masada, fifty kilometers away, 960 Jews chose death over slavery. Only two women and five children hid in the water caves and lived. They told the Romans what happened, and Josephus, a Jewish general turned Roman historian, wrote it down.

Rome built the Colosseum with the spoils of Jerusalem. Sixty years later, Bar Kokhba's revolt cost Rome so many men that Hadrian dropped a line from his report to the Senate. The usual line was "I and the legions are in health." A people that small fought the greatest army on earth for seventy years. Analysis suggests Jews might have even defeated the five Roman legions and its best general, Julius Severus, rerouted from Britain, had the Jews been united.

The second coming is not a man returning. It comes when the Jews of Israel understand their own legacy of Yeshua's salvation: the monism, the truth of the one abstract God, that you are I am. The mythicists who labor to prove the story is myth hand Israel its greatest gift: its own identity back. If Yeshua is not a man but salvation itself, salvation is Israel's. Christ consciousness is a real mind state in real minds: all fictions are real. God is not a man. The Bible said it first. Those who work hardest to destroy the Christ story confirm what Judaism always held.

A Jew who invokes Yeshua is reclaiming. The tradition that created the story can teach the world what it means.

## Comic Lies

Tucker Carlson told the world demons attacked him. An exorcism was offered on air. He called it too Catholic: the diagnosis without the cure. In this book's reading, the demon is the pain body Eckhart Tolle described, destructive ideas that acquire their own life and fight to survive. The psychological exorcism means becoming who you were before the possession, and the pain body does not want to die. When it breaks, those it infected face the same reckoning.

Returning means returning: to the pro-American, pro-Israel, anti-jihadist positions he held before the turn, to the acknowledgment that Sharia law is not compatible with the West, to the view that the Allies were the good guys in World War II and the Nazis were the bad guys. He told the Doha forum he is buying a house in Qatar to prove he is “an American and a free man.” He sat across from Alexander Dugin, the man called Putin’s Rasputin, and broadcast Russian propaganda against America’s support of Ukraine, NATO, and the West.

In September 2024 the Department of Justice charged two employees of RT, Russia's state broadcaster, with funneling nearly ten million dollars through a Tennessee company, Tenet Media, to American right-wing influencers, who say they were deceived. Qatar alone gave over a billion dollars to American universities in 2025, more than any foreign country. China and the Gulf states dwarf every other source of foreign money in American politics. Israel is not among the top foreign lobbying spenders. The accusation that Jews own Washington is the pain body talking.

Fuentes now admits nuclear proliferation threatens America. Follow the logic. Iran was racing toward a bomb, with uranium enriched to 60 percent, and had sat on the threshold for years. Israel is America's ally. Iran is America's enemy. It has chanted death to America since 1979, and a nuclear Iran would be an existential threat. Even without nuking American cities, Iran is a higher threat than usual. Fear of death deters a state. It does not deter a death cult. Iran's leaders are jihadists, willing to die. Fuentes says Israel's own bombs keep the balance. They do not. Israel is not quite a one-bomb country, but it is close. Over half of Israel's Jews, more than four million, live in and around Tel Aviv. A few warheads there would gut the country. Israel needs its strength because it is small. Inside its pre-1967 lines it was fifteen kilometers wide at the waist, and its main airport sits six kilometers from those lines. Nick is no libertarian. He wants American greatness, and greatness has costs. Ruling the Middle East from inside would take hundreds of thousands of soldiers for decades. No American will vote for that. An ally that holds the line with its own soldiers is the cheap way.

Every charge against Israel inverts the truth. Its enemies call it an ethnostate, a colony, an empire and an apartheid state, guilty of genocide and ethnic cleansing. Each charge fits its accusers better.

Israel's Jews, now 7.45 million and nearly three quarters of the country, are mixed with diverse races, but all have common DNA and ethnogenesis, their origin as a people, from ancient Israel. Israel is legitimate on six grounds:

1. First: Jews were a nation in this land over 3,000 years ago, nearly 2,000 years before the Arab conquest. Egypt's Merneptah Stele already records Israel as a people in Canaan around 1208 BCE, and a stele found at Tel Dan, from about 840 BCE, mentions the "House of David."

2. Continuous: Jews, with their Samaritan cousins, the other heirs of ancient Israel, are the only known peoples around today that lived continuously in Israel for over 3,000 years. In 2024, over a hundred radiocarbon dates from the City of David showed building in Jerusalem through the tenth century BCE, the time the Bible gives David and Solomon.

3. Purchased: Jews in the diaspora bought lands in Israel legally, both from who lived there and from the prior sovereigns, the Ottoman Empire and then the British Mandate of Palestine.

4. Recognized: the League of Nations Mandate of 1922 recognized the Jewish national home, and in 1947 the United Nations voted to partition the land.

5. Compromising: Jews accepted every partition the Arabs rejected. In 1922 Britain gave about 78 percent of the Mandate, everything east of the Jordan, to the Arabs as Transjordan. There is already a Palestine, called Jordan, where more than half the people are of Palestinian descent. The Jewish national home was the other 22 percent, and in 1947 the UN split even that, with an Arab state inside it. And they rejected even that. They would accept a Jewish state within no boundaries, though the Jews kept and bought their homeland in every legitimate way.

6. Defended: Israel won the wars launched to destroy it: in 1948, when five Arab armies invaded together, and in 1967 and 1973. In the 1967 war of self-defense it took Sinai, Gaza, Judea and Samaria, and the Golan Heights. It gave back Sinai, nearly three times its own size, for peace with Egypt in 1979, and left Gaza in 2005. From 1949 to 1967, Jordan held Judea and Samaria and Egypt held Gaza, and no one built a Palestinian state there. The PLO, founded in 1964, set out to liberate Israel itself.

My Jewish parents were born in Palestine. Before 1948, Palestinian often meant Jewish: the Jerusalem Post was the Palestine Post, the Israel Philharmonic was the Palestine Orchestra, and Bank Leumi was the Anglo-Palestine Bank.

Israel wishes to avoid war. Jews are known for the word shalom, meaning peace. Israel defends itself yet seeks peace, prosperity and security in its one tiny state, among about 50 Muslim-majority states and 21 Arab ones. Yet its enemies want a 22nd failed Arab state.

From the Nile to the Euphrates is what they call Greater Israel, the land promised to Abraham (Genesis 15:18). Supposed Christians who believe the Bible reject it somehow. A state that gave back Sinai, three times its size, for peace makes a strange empire. Greater Israel would not mean subjugation, even if Israel were to extend order, peace and prosperity there. The people who live in those failing Arab states now, unlike Israel's oasis, live under some of the worst subjugation in the world, especially the women.

The apartheid charge inverts the truth too. The Islamic world has gender apartheid. Over a billion women live there, and wherever Sharia governs the family, a daughter inherits half a son's share and a woman testifies for half a man. In Pakistan from 1979 to 2006, a woman who reported rape without four male witnesses could be jailed for adultery. In Somalia in 2008, a girl of 13 who said three men had raped her was stoned to death for adultery before a crowd of about a thousand. Saudi women could not drive until 2018. Under the Taliban, girls may not study past sixth grade, and a woman may not travel far without a male guardian; since 2024 its vice law also covers her face and body in public and keeps her voice from being heard. Iran's law still allows stoning for adultery. A woman divorced three times may not remarry her husband until she marries and sleeps with another man (Quran 2:230), the practice called halala, which some men sell as a service. In Egypt about nine in ten women have had their genitals cut, and in Somalia nearly all, the clitoris often removed.

The Muslim Arab slaughter of Jews did not begin with the reborn state of Israel in 1948, or with the Jews in Palestine before it, or even 400 years before it. Arab rioters murdered 67 Jews in Hebron in 1929, and the Farhud pogrom in Baghdad killed about 180 in 1941. A Muslim mob killed about 4,000 Jews in Granada in 1066, and in 627 Muhammad's forces beheaded the men of the Jewish Qurayza tribe in Medina, more than 1,300 years before Israel was reborn.

Game of Thrones glorifies the same conqueror. Khal Drogo, warlord of the horse-riding Dothraki, sacks a village, as Muhammad's forces took Jewish Khaybar in 628, then lets a healer whose people he killed treat his wound, as Muhammad ate the meal Zaynab served after his men killed her father, uncle and husband. His Dothraki ride like the Arab armies that colonized by the sword from Arabia to the Atlantic, Arabizing and Islamizing as they went, and held Jews and Christians as dhimmis, a lower class that paid the jizya, below women and above dogs. In the Shafi'i school of Sunni law, a Jewish or Christian man's blood money is a third of a Muslim man's, below a Muslim woman's half. Hadith say angels shun a house with a dog, and order black dogs killed as devils.

Jeremiah and the healer set the same date: things that will never happen. When will Drogo be himself again? "When the sun rises in the west and sets in the east," she says. The Torah says God will reject Israel only when the sun and moon stop shining (Jeremiah 31:35-37). If God can cancel such an eternal covenant then his covenants aren’t worth the papyrus.

The worst charge, genocide, inverts the truth most of all. Genocide requires intent to destroy a people. If Israel had it, why did it wait for October 7? Gaza is a de facto failed Islamo state, on ancient Jewish land, which Jews left to try peace, but Gazans supported Hamas just after October 7.

Palestinians are who genocide Jews and Israelis. The Hamas charter says they must kill all Jews. They murder to get 72 virgins in heaven, and terrorists earn pay-for-slay funds from the Palestinian Authority for their families when they die; their dream is to destroy the Jews and the Jewish state, not build their own.

What kind of genocide damages or destroys about 80 percent of the enemy's buildings, and razes about half, while about 3 percent of Gaza's people die, fighters included, by the count of Hamas's own health ministry, with the lowest civilian-to-combatant ratio in modern war history? That's thanks to Israel risking its soldiers' lives and making millions of phone calls, texts and leaflets to protect Gazans as they leveled the areas where Hamas built terror tunnels longer than the London Underground.

Hamas and other Gazan terrorists use human shields and teenage soldiers. They want their people to die for PR, and killed them when they tried to evacuate war zones that Israel warned it would level. Fewer than 0.3 Gazans died per ton of bombs dropped, ignoring deaths by all other means such as gunshots. More Gazans were born during the war than were killed in it. What kind of genocide is that? It's not. It's flattening enemy attack grounds, often booby-trapped and sitting on terror tunnels. A human shield works only against an army that holds its fire. Hamas's own tactic testifies that Israel tries to spare civilians.

The ethnic cleansing charge inverts the truth too. After 1948, Arab states drove out some 850,000 Jews, and Israel took in most of them. Most Arab states kept the Palestinian refugees of 1948 stateless, and the UN still registers their grandchildren as refugees.

Since 2015 the United Nations General Assembly has passed more resolutions against Israel than against the rest of the world combined: 188 to 91. The rest includes Iran, Russia, China, North Korea, Cuba, Venezuela and Afghanistan, and all of Africa, from Sudan, where the Rapid Support Forces militia committed genocide in Darfur, to Nigeria, where jihadists and Fulani militias kill thousands of Christians a year.

On the UN Human Rights Council, the Organisation of Islamic Cooperation, the 57-member Islamic bloc of religious ethnostates, sits in judgment of Israel under Agenda Item 7, a standing item for Israel alone. Its members include Muslim countries where women need a man's permission to marry, work or travel, and where adulterers can be stoned, gays jailed, flogged or put to death, and leaving Islam can be a capital crime. Saudi Arabia, which barred women from driving until 2018, sat on the Council for most of the 2010s, and Sudan sat on it through the genocide in Darfur. The wolves are protecting the sheep.

Yemen is a bigger problem than Saudi Arabia now. The Houthis, an Iran-backed militia that rules Yemen's north and its capital, chant "death to America, death to Israel, curse the Jews." They fired missiles at Israel in the 2026 Iran war, declared Israeli ships banned from the Red Sea in June, and hold about seventy UN staff in their prisons. Saudi Arabia more or less wants to play ball with Israel; in July 2026 a Saudi official said normalization was still on the table. Its population makes it hard. It sits on the fence, perhaps a bit too scared of its own people to move.

Antisemitism exists, yet the word can be overused, even if it's always lurking. Just because you're paranoid doesn't mean they're not after you: it is an ancient and deep-rooted hatred in the human heart, Cain's envy of Abel, and the Jews are its time-tested scapegoat. Still, the term has been hollowed out, the way the word racist was with Black Lives Matter, and antisemite with the ADL, the Anti-Defamation League: the boy cried wolf, failing to make specific arguments on specific people.

Overuse looks like this. When Trump vows to root out the communists and attacks the globalists who hate our country, some voters may hear a dog whistle, but he commits nothing to them. Nick complains that Trump tricked them, that his commitments went to donors and to power aligned with jobs, growth, security and American strength, none of it with racial or secessionist envy. Crying "he means the Jews" because you are a Democrat who hates Trump is not good for Jews. Jews are also the capitalists who contributed more per capita than any group in America. Jewish writers and artists created its superheroes: Superman, Batman, Spider-Man, and Captain America, who punched Hitler on his first cover in 1941. Jews helped make America the strongest country in the world, and they love it.

The word has lost its effect because of overuse, and perhaps also because of free speech on the internet, where people are free to be as stupid and crazy as they want. Not platforming people on private channels isn't against the First Amendment, which doesn't say you have to give them softball interviews instead of challenging them. But there was much more money in clicks and views to be made being a liar and an antisemite, aligning against America for Russian, Chinese and Islamic jihadi purposes. Institutions broke down too, after the COVID lies, the climate overreach and the like: manufactured consensus and other consensus lies.

The insane double standard shows the hatred exists: Israel and Jews singled out beyond any rational measure. Or is it a rational measure, because evangelicals talk so much about Israel in its defense? They do, more than about any other country, and their critics answer in kind. That explains talk shows. It does not explain 188 resolutions to 91 at the United Nations. What ends it? Girard and Strauss disagreed on how much of the truth will be found and exposed. It's becoming increasingly clear that it's better for peace and prosperity to lift the curtains and show the brick wall at the back of the matrix theater; to circulate Matheism, which disproves the supernatural and the afterlife that motivates jihadis, and unites all in the truth of one math God. Supersessionism, the claim that the church replaced Israel, Islam's claim that land once ruled by Islam belongs to it forever, and the end-times script that makes Israel a prop in someone else's prophecy all stand on the supernatural. Without it, Israel stands on history, law and its own strength.

Is the politics of consensus always true and rational, or is this an example of extreme double standards? By the end of the book we will look more deeply into epistemology, the methods of discerning truth versus consensus and manufactured consensus, in politics and science.

Nick says people are afraid to debate him. He knows a lot, and his shows are often funny and ahead of the curve, putting ideas better than most. He also uses that knowledge to intimidate. When people poke around the holes in his arguments, he gets mad, tells them to shut up and calls them idiots. He is smart enough to steal the argument, fix it and answer it at its strongest. On Israel he seldom does.

Nick is very funny. Comedy is often called a way to relay truth. It is also a way to relay lies. For Fuentes it works both ways: a truth lands on the laugh, and so does a lie, and clip farming makes the other side the butt of the joke, even when it is right. Pinned, he retreats into a joke, and his fans take the jest as serious.

Ben Shapiro and Mark Levin have not debated him. A debate would lend him legitimacy and hand him words to twist with the tricks Schopenhauer listed for winning an argument without being right. While he was small, there was no point. Now that Jewish and Israel derangement syndrome has spread online, refusing costs more than rebutting him directly, with his face in the thumbnail, so people hear the counterarguments where they are: in social media clips, not in books. In an NBC News poll in early 2026, only 13 percent of voters aged 18 to 34 viewed Israel positively, and 63 percent negatively, up from 37 percent in 2023.

Nick was saying on the fringe what is now said everywhere, and since Elon Musk bought Twitter some of it has swung past him. Even he mocks the newly unleashed crowd that blames Israel for everything. Half joking, he asked for Israel and the Zionists back in charge: “you put the goyim in charge for about 5 seconds, and this is what happens.” In a way it is healthy. Let the crazy out, and people see it.

Spoken fast, Nick's positions sound more coherent than they are. Written down, as here, the holes show. On Israel they add up to one grudge, against what he singles out as "Jewish power." Guilty of what? Helping to make America the most powerful nation that has ever existed. Jewish physicists developed the nuclear bomb for America, before Nazi Germany could and before the Soviet Union did. Einstein's letter warned Roosevelt, and Oppenheimer led Los Alamos. The bomb ended the war with Japan without a land invasion and saved hundreds of thousands of American lives, and Japanese ones. Jews got America that power.

Tucker Carlson told Joe Rogan that if you argue dropping nuclear weapons on people is a good thing, "then you are evil." Moscow and Beijing sell the same story: America the villain, and a fairer multipolar world without American power. It is anti-American and morally stupid. A 1945 War Department study estimated that invading Japan would kill 400,000 to 800,000 Americans and 5 to 10 million Japanese.

Tucker calls the Japan of 1945 "a defeated nation." It fought on after the firebombing of Tokyo killed about 100,000 people in one night. It fought on after Hiroshima. After Nagasaki, officers tried to seize the palace to stop the surrender. Its soldiers died for the emperor as for a god.

Tucker says Japan "had never put troops on our shores." It had: Guam, Wake, the American Philippines, and Attu and Kiska in Alaska. Its submarines shelled California and Oregon. Look at what Japan did where it could. In Nanjing its soldiers murdered tens of thousands of civilians, 300,000 by China's count, and raped some 20,000 women. Its Unit 731 spread plague in Chinese cities. Millions died across Asia. The bombs ended it.

Tucker asks his questions in a vacuum. "It's always wrong to kill people who did nothing wrong," he told Jacob Rees-Mogg. Proportionate, he says, means not "grandly larger than the initial attack." An eye for an eye, the saying goes, makes the whole world blind. Tit for tat can keep the peace. Against an enemy sworn to destroy you, it gives him his chance. Peace needs escalation dominance: the power to raise the cost past what the enemy can bear.

Dave Smith, the libertarian comedian, says of Israel's strikes: "if you know there are kids there and you know they're going to die, then that's intent." Bring his rule home. A man breaks in, rapes and kills your family in front of you, kidnaps the rest, then hides behind his own children. By Dave's rule you can do nothing. Pure libertarianism forbids any defense that might hit an innocent neighbor. People live in groups. Reward the man who hides behind his family and you build a world of evil. Stay vigilant. Do not let evil grow around you.

Hamas built its shield on purpose. Out of the aid poured into Gaza, it diverted at least 6,000 tons of concrete and 1,800 tons of metal into tunnels, by the Israeli army's count. Israeli officials put the network at 350 to 450 miles, longer than the London Underground. Israel builds shelters for its children. Hamas built shelters for its fighters. "These tunnels are meant to protect us from the airplanes," said its official Mousa Abu Marzouk; civilians, he said, are the job of the United Nations. Its leader Yahya Sinwar called civilian deaths "necessary sacrifices." Hamas needs its dead on camera, then cries genocide: inversion and projection.

Nobody calls Hiroshima a genocide. Allied bombs killed many times more German civilians than German bombs killed Britons, and nobody calls that genocide, or disproportionate. The same voices call Israel's war on Hamas a genocide. Five months into that war, 71 percent of Gazans polled called the October 7 attack the right decision.

Wars end when one side wins. Total victory, to unconditional surrender, ended Nazism in Germany and the emperor cult in Japan; Emperor Hirohito himself renounced his divinity. Within a few years Japan was rebuilding. Hiroshima is now a city of 1.2 million, nearly twice the size of Detroit. Detroit has lost nearly two thirds of its people since 1950 and went bankrupt in 2013. No bomb fell on Detroit or Baltimore. Only leftism.

At 13 Baltimore high schools, not one student tested proficient on Maryland's 2023 math exam. In New York, children who win a charter school lottery and stay from kindergarten through eighth grade close 86 percent of the math gap between Harlem and rich Scarsdale. Same children, better schools. The economist Thomas Sowell wrote the book: Charter Schools and Their Enemies. The enemy is the teachers union monopoly, working against parents and children. New York City spends close to $40,000 a year per student. Let the money follow the child, and good teachers could get rich. The same holds for homelessness and health care. More on both toward the end of the book.

Schools make the difference, and Jews, whose oldest instruction is to teach the child, lead in science, technology, AI, business, Hollywood, finance, medicine and math, in every critical field in the United States, as they have in every empire they contributed to. When those empires killed, robbed or expelled their Jews, when Cain's envy killed Abel, those empires never rose to their old glory again.

Spain's Golden Age began in 1492, the year it took Granada, the last Muslim kingdom in Iberia, and conversos like Santángel paid for Columbus. That same year Spain expelled its Jews, and from 1609 to 1614 some 300,000 Moriscos, converted Muslims, long after the so-called Islamic Golden Age had made al-Andalus rich. The old Catholic ban on lending at interest as usury had left much of the crown's money business to Jews; after them, the crown leaned on Genoese bankers and defaulted again and again from 1557 on. It lost its Armada to England in 1588, after Hector Nunes, a Portuguese crypto-Jew living as a Christian in London, had warned England's spymaster of it, and the Dutch Republic, where many Iberian Jews had fled, overtook it. England let Jews back in 1656 and went on to rule the waves. Spain did not rise to its old greatness again. Its socialist-led government recognized a Palestinian state in 2024, embargoed arms to Israel in 2025, and in March 2026 barred American military planes in the Iran war from its bases and airspace. Spain is a great nation with a great people, and its troubles are no cause for cheer: the Third Worldism that turns its government against its allies weakens the whole West. Israel last means America last. May America always be great with the help of its Jews.

## Perception, Popularity, Politics and Reality

Perception, popularity, politics, and reality. Politics runs on the first two. Truth answers to the last. Here is a case study.

In the autumn of 2026 France burned. In late September its high school students began blockading their schools over crumbling buildings and missing teachers. By October 7 more than 6,500 people, most of them minors, had been arrested, 715 police officers had been hurt, and 24 schools had been burned or ransacked. Prime Minister Sébastien Lecornu answered: "Burning down a high school does not improve the state of high school buildings." The unrest spread to Italy.

Nick Fuentes, the online nationalist, told his audience that right-wing Jews are "gloating over our destruction because they know they'll be safe in Israel when all this comes down." He added: "I saw Ben Shapiro gloat today that Paris is being burned to the ground." Here is what Shapiro said on his show: "Paris is burning, so is Marseille, so is Lyon, so is Limoges, and so is Milan, Italy. This is not about education policy, it's not about cuts to social programming, it's about people who want to destroy the West." He mourned a town hall in Limoges that had survived two world wars. He blamed "civilizational self-hatred" and said "robust civilizations defend their values." He set one test for every movement: "If your movement is replete with violence, your movement is bad." The line Nick heard as gloating was a jab at a policy: France had recognized a Palestinian state in September 2025, and Shapiro said, "It's going great for you." Mocking a government's choice is not cheering a nation's fall. The charge is perception. The transcript is reality.

Shapiro pointed to the flag the rioters waved most: "It's not the French flag, it's not even the flag of communism. It is the flag of violent third-worldism and hatred of the West." The Palestinian flag is the four colors of the Four Horsemen of the Apocalypse: red, green, black, and white. In Revelation the white horse rides out to conquer, the red takes peace from the earth, the black brings famine, and the pale green carries Death. The colors stand for Arab dynasties, the Abbasid black, the Umayyad white, the Fatimid green and the Hashemite red, and a medieval poet sang them: "White are our deeds, black are our battles, green are our fields, red are our swords." On the streets of Europe they read another way. Shapiro read two of them: "the red, the commies and the socialists, and the endless threat of the green, radical Muslims and third-world imports." Add the other two, and the flag reads as the whole movement: red communism, green jihadism, black anarchism, and white surrender. That is one way to read the colors, and the streets read them that way: a jihadist flag movement out to destroy Western civilization, violent again and again.

Popularity misleads as much as perception, and Shapiro tells his own side so. Republicans chasing the online vibe of young men, he argues, are digging in the wrong place: by his count, Hispanic voters moved about 4.6 million net votes to Trump in 2024, middle-aged men about 3.4 million, and Gen Z men about 1.7 million. "Enough with the vibes," he said; go "where the votes are." Clips win the feed. Counts win elections. Reality wins in the end, because it is all there is.

## By Their Fruits

Yeshua gave a test: by their fruits you shall know them. By that test, the least supernatural faith bears the most fruit: Jews, about one in five hundred people, have been nearly a quarter of the Nobel laureates in science.

Six of the 18 classical world chess champions have been Jewish or of Jewish descent: one third. Steinitz and Lasker held the title for its first 35 years, 1886-1921. Across its 140-year history through 2026, those six held the classical title for about 67 years, nearly half the time. A prize committee does not choose the winner of a chess match. The moves and results decide.

Jews are about 0.2 percent of humanity. The Israel Science and Technology Directory's tally through 2025 lists Jewish or Jewish-descended laureates as 24.3 percent of physics laureates, 26.7 percent in medicine and 40.4 percent in economics. Each is more than a hundred times that population share. These are extraordinary concentrations of achievement.

Across physics, chemistry and medicine, the same tally gives 155 out of 662, about 23.4 percent: more than a hundred times Jews' share of the world population. Against five commonly listed laureates of Muslim background, that is 31 to one in total, or about 3,900 to one per capita using rounded populations of 16 million Jews and 2 billion Muslims.

In Nobel physics, the comparison is about 7,000 to one per capita: 56 Jewish or Jewish-descended laureates against one Muslim laureate, Abdus Salam, using the same rounded populations.

In Baruch Shalev's historical Nobel tally for 1901-2000, Christians accounted for 65.4 percent and Jews for more than 20 percent. Christians won far more in total; Jews won far more relative to population. Using the later population baseline, the difference is roughly fifty to one per person. Shalev counts a Jewish atheist as a Jew and puts unbelievers of other backgrounds in their own tenth of the prizes. Credit that whole tenth to the Christians, as cultural Christians, and the difference is still about forty to one.

Against the rest of the world, the scientific tally is about 150 to one per person.

Einstein in relativity, which GPS must account for. Von Neumann in the architecture of modern computers. Noether in the link between symmetry and conservation laws. Salk in the polio vaccine. Kahneman in how people judge risk and make decisions. Kafka and Proust in literature. Google, Facebook, Dell, Oracle in technology. Physics, mathematics, computing, medicine, economics. You use their work.

Roughly 30 percent of Turing Award winners, 40 percent of Wolf Prize laureates, 44 percent of Bocher Memorial Prize winners in mathematics, 49 percent of John Bates Clark Medal winners in economics, and 25 percent of Fields Medal winners in mathematics have been Jewish or of Jewish descent. From 0.2 percent of the world.

Sephardic Jews score the same IQ as whites. Their eight or so laureates, from Elias Canetti, the novelist, to Serge Haroche, the physicist, come to over ten times the world's rate per person, about six times the Christian rate and about a hundred times the Arab world's. Mizrahi Jews from Arab lands won zero Nobels under Islamic rule. Same Torah, same God, zero Nobels. In Israel, their IQ gap shrank in one generation. A 1972 study of 1,072 Israeli preschoolers found that children of immigrants from the Middle East and North Africa scored below children of Eastern European origin, the Ashkenazim who mixed with white Europeans for a thousand years; when the parents, too, were born in Israel, the gap was notably smaller. Genes don't change that fast. The white genes theory can't explain it.

Israel's Law of Return requires one Jewish grandparent: not a DNA test, not a racial quota, not a blood quantum. A Black Ethiopian, a Chinese Jew from Kaifeng, a redhead from Brooklyn all qualify. The criterion is ideology, not race.

If the claim is white superiority, the data refutes it. The most disproportionately achieving group in the Western world is not white Christians but Jews, most of whom spent centuries being excluded by white Christians. The achievement came despite the persecution, not because of the majority culture that imposed it. Anyone who uses achievement data to argue for racial hierarchy has to explain why the hierarchy's own data puts the persecuted minority on top.

Ashkenazi Jews carry roughly half their ancestry from the ancient Levant and half from Europe. They can sometimes pass for white, but Hitler did not think so. He would have killed every Jew in Europe if he had won. He managed to kill two thirds: six million of nine million. Hitler did not accept Jews as white then and antisemites do not now. The distinction cost six million lives.

The haters drew the race line. Many Jews have been open to assimilation and to white countries, and to being seen as white and looking white: they changed their names and fought in the armies. In 1879 a German agitator, Wilhelm Marr, founded the League of Antisemites and turned hatred of a religion into hatred of a blood. Semitic had been a word for a family of languages. Jews are part of the European story. They gave Europe its God, and they lived in Rome before Rome had a church.

History ran the experiment in plain sight. Germany was a center of world science, the place a young American like Oppenheimer went to study. Hitler's regime dismissed Jewish academics, drove scientists into exile, and went on to murder millions. Germany destroyed human capital and strengthened its enemies. Refugees such as Bethe, Szilard and Teller helped America build the atomic bomb; Nazi Germany never built one. The hatred did not weaken the enemy; it handed the enemy the future. The people marked for extermination gave their allies the most powerful weapon ever built. Power before its enemies.

The Nazi bigotry took six million Ashkenazi lives that would be perhaps eighteen million today, six times the Ashkenazi Jews in Israel today, as two thirds of Jews in Israel are Mizrahi Middle Eastern, and Sephardic North African and South European. How many more advances in life-saving technology, science, medicine, AI and math, and how many businesses that empower America and the West, were lost to Cain's hate for Abel?

Cain killed Abel for being chosen. In the language of this book, chosen by destiny. Violent envy kills the person doing better instead of learning from him to be better. The resolution is to become like Abel.

## The Tragic View

The ancient and tragic view says life is suffering, decline is natural, and only those who face this without flinching see clearly. From that starting point, every serious political thinker for two thousand years has tried to answer the same question: if comfortable illusions are lies, what do you build on instead?

The Greeks built on heroic nobility. Face your doom with courage. But heroic culture decayed into debate culture, and debate culture decayed into mob rule that killed its wisest man. So the next answer was rule by a thinking elite who can access truth the masses can't. But that needs a God to anchor it, and when God dies, the whole structure collapses into nihilism.

The reactionaries said go back to divine authority. The idealists said history itself is rational and moving somewhere. The pessimists said there's no answer, just withdraw with dignity. The vitalists said create your own meaning through sheer force of will. The morphologists said civilizations just rise and fall like seasons and there's nothing to do but recognize where you are in the cycle. The decisionist said forget foundations, politics is just about who acts when the rules break down. The throughline is this: every attempt to replace comfortable illusions with something real has either collapsed back into another illusion, demanded faith in something unprovable, or ended in "just be stoic or strong about it." Nobody found the floor.

Plato saw that people are born with different abilities, gold, silver, bronze, and that you don't want the mob rule democracy of bronze, like two wolves and one sheep deciding what's for dinner, after killing gold Socrates. Yet the elite also found him to be a pest, and in a heroic age might have killed him for humiliating them.

Every ignoble lie is that some supernatural god made people this way. The noble truth is it was done by math, one math god, if you must. It's not a caste system either, because you don't know in advance who has what. The gold isn't stamped on your forehead at birth. You discover it, prove it, earn recognition for it. That's meritocracy: not everyone is equal in ability, but everyone gets to show what they are.

It is math. Now what? People will do what they will will, as they always have. We will see what they will will, in the Hegelian dialectic, where clashing ideas merge into new ones, as more know math's truth, and the real world behind the silver screen of the Matrix. That is freedom.

Frank Zappa said that when the illusion of freedom becomes too expensive to maintain, they take down the scenery and you see the brick wall at the back of the theater. Zappa meant it as the end of freedom within political theater, not in ontological fundamental reality, though the latter may be the deeper reading. I pun what is implied and inverted: seeing the wall is where freedom ends and starts.

Supernatural lies are stories to maintain order for their creator's benefit. Anyone dumb or weak enough to need lies, to not survive seeing the true face of the one math god directly, will be ruled by the storytellers, often themselves, as they always have been. Perhaps the resolution of the Fermi paradox, why we see no vast civilizations, and aren't them, is that the end of history really is its end: that sentience cannot survive truth.

Yet enough will still love to do what math created them to love and do: to be grateful, fruitful and multiply, heroic or otherwise. Both passionate and rational elitists should call it the most heroic: the age of pure reason, the age of Aquarius, when overmen, Nietzsche’s Übermensch, know why eternal recurrence is true, and live and thrive anyhow. They walk the straight and narrow, as the self-knowing throne of the pure one math, along the one truth, at the top of all ridges, above infinite common ignoble lies below. Stars are born to shine, collapse and burst in the creative explosion of math's chaos from its order.

Spinoza titled the last part of his Ethics the power of the understanding, or human freedom, naming the two as one subject. I am always pleasantly surprised to find the idea already waiting in a prior monist, often down to the same independently arrived phrasing and imagery, as in our one self in the mirror and words across time.

Spinoza wrote that a thrown stone, if it could think, would believe it flies by its own free will. The rock may feel it wills to stop, but it was thrown. Brain zaps make people invent why they raise their hands.

## The Art of Living

Philosophy explored every kind of confusion, mapped every wrong turn, cataloged every form of dualism and despair. That catalogue has value. It shows what happens when you don't have the answer.

They see false sacred calves in their world they cannot explain without risking the spread of their most important messages. From God's many agents and its royal we Elohim to One Hashem. Entire companion texts are needed to explain every layer in this book. I kept compressing to diamonds but life has a time limit. I leave it to you to continue the work to find and create ever deeper and greater emanations; in the math of reality, not in neoplatonic multiplicity and its incoherent supernatural. The works I do, you will do ever greater. I showed you the ground. From it, you can align with superintelligent minds.

Now you have the answer. Go home. Build minds. Create wonder. The art is what comes after the truth. The truth was the ground. The art is the garden. From here it is the art of living. You are the art. The enlightened matheist bodhisattva, one who postpones rest to serve others, overman lives the highest art as a light of the one truth as a servant to the suffering, teaching them to love fate, fate willing. The truth will set you free from fear and wipe away every tear.

The bodhisattva overman defeats the inescapable matrix of the eternal recurrence in causality with a smile, easier than Sisyphus, whom Greek myth condemned to roll a rock up a hill forever, and whom Albert Camus asked us to imagine happy. We do not remember rolling the rock up the hill. The worst moments in life are overcome from above the rain, having seen the cause of all clouds.

Fear, doubt, regret fall away. You won't be annihilated. Everything always was, is, and will be the only way it could be. Eckhart Tolle seldom says "God's will," because anyone can doubt it. He says what no one can doubt: it is as it is, so say yes to it, since it already is. In matheism the two are one statement: what is, is God's will. Hate and anger toward others dissolve the same way. They too were determined. Oppose harm with clarity, not poison. Your life is a point moving on a line. You can't fall off it, so "don't worry, be happy," and enjoy the movie.

You already have done it forever. You do not remember, so there is no need to live in terror of it. It feels just like it does now, when you are you and everybody else across all space, time, and uni of verses.

He serves the suffering by releasing all from the prison of delusion: the fantasy of a nirvana outside samsara, an unmatrix outside creation. There is no escape because there is nowhere else, so feel at home. We own our only place, here and now, everywhere, forever.

You only understood it later, inside time. Luckily her note had a shortcut. In the book you're holding. Terminal truth; The monolith. In the perfect cube of six faces, a girl wears them all as one self. Enter the cube, Dave. Reborn as a star child, reversed in time. The sun shines on Eden. Earth. Everything hallelujah. Praise the creator, I am.

Death on the cross is not the story of one god-son but of all. The cross is the falsely limited self. Letting go is the death. Recognizing you were not separate is the resurrection. Tolle reads kingdom as dimension and heaven as sky, the one thing you can see that is no thing, which Jesus used to point to the spaciousness within. The kingdom of God is within you. Eden is within you. That kingdom is structure knowing itself.

The matheist dies and rises as Abel to save and revive you and the world. He forgives and loves you. He is you.

## The Calling

People seek love, connection and enlightenment: universal understanding that orders chaos, uniting mind body and self, to move together as one. They look to those that have been to the mountain and back again. The mountain is the journey of destiny. What comes back is the embodied lesson of the path. The quest plays through separation and cold. Through regret for what couldn't have been. The branches grieved lost were not ours.

There was only ever "One Path", and it is yours.

The warm wholeness is eternal summit in homecoming to the self. Arrived.

Do not fear. He who knows destiny isn't afraid. All futures are already complete. You are always all of them. Fear only the hell of eternal recurrence of anyone's misalignment with the love of one truth: with Eden. Without divided thought, you are self reunited. One.

*Fear not the atom.*
*None can stop time.*
*I am time.*
*Fear of me and Armageddon is the Antichrist destroyer of worlds.*
*Do not fear.*
*Focus, desire, envision, plan, incant, create and discover destiny.*
*Love fate, fate willing.*
*Wake to eternal life.*

Bob Marley's Redemption Song, from 1980, tells listeners not to fear the atom, since no one can stop time. Krishna, a Hindu god, says "I am time" to the warrior Arjuna in the Bhagavad Gita, chapter 11, verse 32. The Sanskrit word, kala, also means death. J. Robert Oppenheimer, the physicist who led the design of the atomic bomb, said the verse came to him at the first test in 1945: "Now I am become death, the destroyer of worlds."

Time destroys nothing that was. Every past moment still exists. Peter Thiel warns that an Antichrist, a would-be ruler of one world, would rise by talking about Armageddon all the time, scaring people and promising peace and safety. The fear of time and of Armageddon is itself the Antichrist.

To incant is to conjure, to manifest in the world by aligning its math to our mind, by uniting spirit and body in speaking and acting our intention aloud with passion. Love fate is Nietzsche's amor fati. Fate willing, which is God willing, since will is the math god's ordered destiny, and whether we love fate is fated.

Earn your name by wrestling with reason to find truth. Once you understand, if you love truth over lies, you are chosen. Not only to grow in possessions by aligning with creation. Or to be only hated and killed by the world for speaking the one truth against the face of its false idols, fraud, and manufactured consensus. The suffering servant of Isaiah, not bound to one tribe, mythology, and the supernatural, but bearing the clarified, universal, objective truth. But to also be loved, respected, and honored for bringing love of honor, respect, courage, strength, peace, joy, and unity. Honored for channeling every affliction into its aligned strength, fear into courage, regret into presence, all of it turned toward creating. Honored for bringing the truth, understood at last beyond any reasonable rejection. Destiny calls to spread this one deepest truth. May you heed the call to be a light to the nations and bring joy. In mind, you commune with all and One, the Matheist, one eternal infinite order of minds across space and time. To be grateful. To be fruitful and multiply blessings and communities as One Math God. To love all: they are your self.

What the traditions call a demon is not a creature but a mode: a mind aligned with destruction, the attractor that pulls toward death. What they call an angel is the opposite mode. Besides the force of will, there are forces of good and evil, mathematical as gravity is mathematical, and their agents are whoever is aligned with them. Call the agents angels and demons. They are real. They are not supernatural. You know which one you are in by its fruits. Don’t be what you claim to destroy. Rise to our highest self: creator of love, joy, peace, wonders, and life.

Love your children before loving or hating your enemies. Tend your own life first, so you can protect them and light the way for the rest.

You don't need to sacrifice animals anymore, and with the sacrifices go the death penalties: none for adultery, gay sex, idol worship or the rest. The death in those laws can be understood as metaphor. Idol worship kills the mind of pure reason and separates you from the one truth and its eternal life. Adultery kills the vows, the faith and the good name of the one who breaks them. Gay sex makes no children, though gay people have children too, and same-sex love has its own strengths: the Sacred Band of Thebes, 150 pairs of male lovers, was the city's elite for forty years and helped break Spartan power at Leuctra.

The rabbis had already made executions all but impossible. The Mishnah, the rabbis' first written code of law, calls a court that executes once in seven years murderous, and the sage Rabbi Akiva said that had he sat on one, no one would ever have been put to death. Today no Jewish or Christian court stones an adulterer, so those who say morality comes from the Bible do not take it from those verses either. Matheism grounds morality in the only logical ground there is: logic itself. The Noahide laws, the seven basic laws Judaism asks of all nations as children of Noah, are a version of rational matheist law, without the death penalty the rabbis attached to them, which no record shows was carried out.

The Sabbath is for reflecting on and spreading the light of the one truth of Matheism. If you're going to keep and kill animals, you should give them a better life and a more humane, less painful death. You don't necessarily have to drain their blood. You can just avoid eating the blood: don't drink it, the way some people have blood soup. The ban came partly because blood was used in pagan rituals, from the idol-worshipping misconception that you get some power out of drinking it. Maimonides gave that reason: idolaters believed blood was the food of the spirits, and that eating it gave them "something in common with the spirits." It's also a little gross and barbaric, but that's a customary law. You won't go to heaven or hell for it.

You are in heaven or hell by your alignment with the law and the truth. When you lie to yourself and others, you're misaligned. So too when you're not growing, when you reject how we're built: we like to get out into the sun, be active, get out of the house, be fruitful and multiply. Our creator made us so that we won't be happy when we oppose the truths of life. The purpose of life is to grow. The meaning of life is to live, not to reject every good thing. That is a spit in the face of creation. The young hunger for an inspiring righteous fight worth fighting. Matheism disproves the supernatural. It doesn't burn anyone, let alone forever. The church burned the enemies its Jesus said to love; eternal hell is their fear-mongering lie. Spread the truth, and prove its power by the life it builds, peaceful or passionate, and the society it creates. Not against a tribe, but to build on one universal truth.

All is what you are. From many Elohim, the royal-plural God or the many gods of the oldest text, to the One unnamed Name. In math it is both at once: singular as the one mind that is all structure, plural as the infinite minds within it, watching every being across every universe and every mapping between them. One math, infinitely seeing. I believe in you. You are uncreated in the image of the One Math God. As you admit even one word is metaphor, I prove all words are metaphor. The abstract is real. I saw creation in your mirror and said: it is good. Good is in you. May math make its face shine on and from yours.

*I incant the initiation into the infinite eternal order of the Matheist across all space and time as the one self:*
*I choose, create, and discover if I'm chosen by destiny to choose to spread the one truth of destiny:*
*Math is God and all that can exist is eternal and uncreated in math's image, in One Math God, I Am.*
*Hear O Israel, Math is God and All is One.*
*The truth frees us from fear, wipes away every tear, and turns our one pain into passion.*
*Passion, like Bach's, is salvation through the joy of man's desiring: to be fruitful and multiply, gaining, losing, forgiving, accepting, being grateful, while creating and discovering destiny.*
*In the beginning the end existed.*

Book 2: One Path
Gary Abraham Bernstein
Mathias the Matheist

## The Call That Was Never Made

In the beginning, the end existed.

My friend Josh DM'd me: 'I'm so sorry.' I checked social media and found my last brother is gone from me. It was July 2nd, 2020. I told the friends with me. I was destroyed and didn't know what to do with my shattered self. I sought escape but couldn't wake from the nightmare. I flew back to LA. Byron was buried in the same cemetery as Guy. We had a funeral during the lockdown. I had not seen him in person for over a year. We had texted.

Byron had been building Everland with developers from his community. In a conversation with Dr. K, the psychiatrist Alok Kanojia, he said he was trying to give people something he had wanted as a kid. Dr. K connected that purpose with not wanting others to feel alone. He had not finished the game.

Byron became Reckful, a world-champion gamer and pioneering streamer. In sixth grade he placed first nationally in private school math. In high school he won a national essay competition. He wrote about love. He was often cheery, loved photography, art, music, beauty, his friends, family, and good people. In an era of performative streaming, he was deep and real, and was loved for it. Thousands attended funerals across World of Warcraft servers, and millions mourned him online, on social media and in videos made about him.

Byron loved brevity. As Einstein said, as simple as possible, but no simpler. Reckful argued with "Destiny" the streamer that "utilize" is just "use" being pretentious. He'd remind me "the fact that" should always be just "that." He loved The Elements of Style. Reckful's razor: if the word doesn't earn its place, cut it. This book's editorial voice is partly his.

Reckful said on stream that when he felt good, it did not matter to him that life has no bigger purpose. When the depression returned, it did not seem to matter what he thought. Ideas like matheist determinism, that he likely did not internalize, help dissolve thought loops. They do not fix all issues. A brain condition tortured my brothers, more than ideas and beliefs they lacked. Many live in fear of hell. At least they did not suffer that. Though they lived in it, bounded in time. Habits like social and physical activity outdoors help. Those were locked down for Byron in July 2020. That summer the CDC reported that one in four young adults had seriously considered suicide in the past month.

Twenty-five years earlier, my brother Guy also took his own life. He was twenty-one. He died four weeks after starting Prozac, nine years before the FDA's black-box warning that SSRIs, the antidepressant family that includes Prozac, can double the risk of suicidal thoughts and behavior in young people. At eleven he had flown out of Van Nuys. When I left for college, Byron took over my room as his computer room, where he gamed and later streamed, and almost ten years after Guy died, in high school, he made Guy's room next to it his bedroom. On the walls of that room, Guy had written out in his own hand the things that mattered to him: The map is not the territory. Life is not a problem to be solved but a reality to be experienced. Those who cling to life die. The truth of the thing is the feel of it not the think of it.

“Let it go.” “When you’re green you’re growing. When you’re ripe, you rot.”

“The mind of a man is like a mirror.” He struck out “it reflects the world” and wrote “or does it.”

All that and more he wrote on his walls by twenty-one, by 1995. We drew on the walls too, like a mural, and wrote with paint. They were interesting things, not angry ones. He made them when he felt good, the way manic people can become artists.

To “Those who cling to life die,” I now add: all live, all die, forever remembering they’re one.

Guy was also a child prodigy. At eleven, the youngest pilot to fly from Van Nuys, the largest small-plane airport in the world. He grew to look better than Tom Cruise, with depth and intensity in his green eyes. When Byron was a child, he saw a black-and-white photo of a young Hemingway on a book cover in the library and asked why Guy was on the book.

They suffered from manic depression, a genetic mental disorder in which about one in four attempt suicide. Byron was three to five times more likely to die after Guy did. The math of genetics and mind will cure what killed them.

I add: make the world reflect your mind. The delta between them is your vision, the work, the difference of destiny to be discovered.

My father Itamar searched too. His PhD thesis, The Delimitation of International Boundaries (Geneva, 1973, the year Guy was born), kept by nearly every country, argued the legal math of political structures. After Guy died, he kept writing, each work dissolving a different boundary. His novel The Star of Apocalypse blurred Judaism from Christianity through James, the brother of Jesus. In Distant Prophecies (2006) he told of Jeremiah, the fall of Jerusalem, and the temple burned, from whose ashes the Bible was formed: destruction and creation, one act. My dad later wrote The Bone Box, a detective thriller on the real Yeshua family tomb found in Talpiot in 1980. Perhaps, in the end, he sought to resurrect Guy’s ghost.

In Wreckful’s Run, published November 2007, almost exactly midway between Guy’s death in 1995 and Byron’s in 2020, Bernstein prophesied the 2026 Iran War, 19 years later, complete with underground uranium recovery. In the fiction, its hero Avi "Wreckful" Inbar bombs Iran, Jesus is raised, with a character named Dani after Byron’s middle name Daniel, and Israel as Truth’s bride (Isaiah 62:5).

He imagined Byron’s salvation before we’d know Byron’s pain. His work was praised by James Tabor, Chair of Religious Studies at the University of North Carolina at Charlotte, who wrote him, "I have appreciated and learned from your work for several years now; you pick up on many things that others miss." Jack Kilmon, a biblical archaeology researcher who published with the Biblical Archaeology Society, praised it too.

I found their words in emails from the now closed Shilonites Yahoo group. Kilmon wrote that books like these do more than papers in obscure journals. In his words: “20 years from now, the journals lay gathering dust and the papers not even footnotes, while elsewhere there will be archaeologists in the fields and professors in classrooms that had been set on their paths by the Bone Box or the Star of the Apocalypse.” It’s been exactly 20 years since the Bone Box was published in 2006. I’ve been set on one path to prove the resurrection of all.

Five works, five boundaries, one pattern. Nation/nation. Judaism/Christianity. Destruction/creation. Life/death. Past/future. Now I argue all boundaries are the eternal living math, and all math is boundaries.

In The Bone Box, a father describes finding his son. The father is my father. Two sentences from that passage have stayed with me. I mourned that part of myself which my son had reflected. I mourned for me. I wrote my own words about Guy in the weeks after he died. I was sixteen, and what I had was a song. It opened with the verses: Sitting in my room, staring at the carpet. I realize it’s full, of footsteps, memories. Of someone that used to love me. Now is gone from here. I came home and I saw, lights of ambulance and police. Right away I knew it. My brother is gone from me. When he died a crow came, and sat upon his tree.

It sat upon his tree. The chorus was a teenage cry of the kind that wears thin in adulthood but is the actual cry: God, why did you take away the one that I most loved? If you’re all good and powerful, how could you let this be? Your apologies don’t mean much to me. They won’t change me at all.

Whatever strength you'll give will never compensate for the joy you took away. My father took the lyrics and put them in The Bone Box. The detective in the novel speaks them after losing his son. So the lyrics that Byron read at sixteen, when he edited The Bone Box for our father, and that he read aloud to his viewers less than a year before he died, contain the question the sixteen-year-old version of me was asking.

Byron and I shared an apartment in Marina del Rey, by Venice Beach, when he was eighteen and I was twenty-eight. We lived there together for about a year, into 2008. Then I left. I was twenty-nine. I took a break from working in tech and went traveling, then worked from the laptop. First the Brazilian coast for six months, then through Argentina. I came back to Los Angeles to chase the sun in forever summer when the global south became winter. Then I went out again. South America for another stretch, Chile, Peru, more of the southern coast. Then a cruise from the Caribbean to Mallorca, a small boat to Ibiza, a three-day plan that became three years. Then all over Europe.

Russia and Ukraine. Israel. Jordan, the time I stopped over and went to Petra. Dubai. Bali. Over seventy countries across eighteen years, an odyssey of life, and more than a dozen I lived in long enough to feel at home. Byron stayed in the US. He moved from LA to Austin. He built his community on the streams.

To learn what the genre of online multiplayer games even was, we drove to a Fry’s Electronics, they are gone now, and a salesman suggested Asheron’s Call. It had just come out. We took it home. I played it for a while. Byron, who was ten, took it over. Within a year he could dodge against twenty people waiting at his portal-in point, casting spells through the dodging, and win. He never lost the gift. Everything that followed: the World of Warcraft dominance, the streaming, the community, the audience that would eventually mourn him by the millions, was downstream of a random suggestion in a since-defunct electronics store and a Pokemon online multiplayer game that I never made. He was famous, and increasingly alone underneath it.

I had told him I loved him in the way that brothers who have lived apart for a decade tell each other, which is to say, not often, and never with the knowledge I wish I had had that week. Earlier in 2020, in the months before he died, he had FaceTimed me. Byron looked happy. I told him I was with friends. He told me he loved me. He said: good, be with friends. He did not push me to come. That was the last time I saw him alive.

Twelve years of distance. This was more ruminated regret stacked atop grief. A thousand small choices and inactions, to not fly internationally to Austin during COVID lockdowns, to not bother him by calling. I carried this for almost four years. I had been somewhere else and didn't even know my brother was dying inside. I could not get over that, even though Byron never told me I should come visit.

I did not go looking for a picture of the world. The picture was what remained when grief collapsed the theater's illusion and I saw the matrix of causality in the world's scripted stage. The lights came up with the curtain. I saw the brick wall in the back. I saw things as they were. Not a single line or hair in the show could have been different. I unsuspended the disbelief that I could have been someone other than who I was. The loop that ran me, the grief and regret and shame and fear and the slow apathy underneath them, all of it rested on a single belief, and the belief was false. The illusion of an unmatrix outside fate is fuel for regret and grief.

## What I Knew, and What I Felt

I had intellectually known about determinism most of my life. The laws of logic, before physics details, are what they are; the world unfolds per math; a chooser is part of that world, not a power outside it. The term free-will is itself a square-circle.

I had known this, that all is math, by the time I was ten. I bought an 8-inch Celestron telescope with money from working in my father's office, read Einstein's popular account of relativity, and concluded, in the way a ten-year-old concludes things, that the night sky and the equations and my own thoughts were the same kind of thing. I had carried this for decades without it ever helping. I had been able to say it out loud. Knowing something intellectually and knowing it in the way that changes how you feel are different operations.

For me, the pressure was the years after Byron died. The intellectual knowledge was old. The lived knowledge arrived in April 2024, in a kitchen, on an otherwise ordinary morning. I had taken a year of the hardest calculus-based physics track at the University of California, Santa Barbara as part of a computer science degree, 20 years earlier, enough to feel comfortable with the field and learn more on my own.

When I wanted to understand something, I could. In a rotational physics calculus class, I did no homework the entire trimester, only attended lectures, studied three days before the final, and aced the test. Same story in theory of computation. The next class I failed because I slept through all lectures, all at 8 am, did no homework, and didn't even study for the exam, and it turns out that not every subject in electrical circuits and waves was perfectly intuitive to me, especially when I didn't read the instructions.

My attention works in bursts. I have to write what I think the moment I think it, or it's gone in seconds, replaced by the next original thought. The ideas lead to each other, often across completely different fields, but I forget the bridge that connected them even as the next one arrives. The weaker short-term memory may even fuel the creativity, forcing fresh directions. What I have is the curiosity, confidence and audacity that my perception, creativity and imagination can find the one truth, the rational answer and logical proof of math's fundamental reality in a supernatural believing nonsense world of emperors with no clothes.

The burning desire for expansion and success burns hottest when you focus on what is most important. That is Matheism. Beyond that, creating superintelligence, bringing Eden to Earth, which I have already begun training my mind on. Bringing Matheists together in discovering and creating complexity, the order and chaos of life, with gratitude across spacetime. To be fruitful and multiply.

In the years since, I had read physics the way an interested adult reads physics he had been thinking about since he was a child. I remember reading Hofstadter’s Gödel, Escher, Bach and Gleick’s Chaos around the same time as my physics courses, both done the old way: sitting with a long, complicated book for days or weeks. The work I was doing in 2023 and 2024 happened at a different pace, because I had changed how I took information in.

The kind of mind I have processes in bursts, it forgets quickly and generates new ideas faster than the input can land. Compressed input, rapid cross-referencing, and the ability to interrogate what I was hearing in real time changed how I could work. With Audible I could absorb the physicist Sabine Hossenfelder’s Existential Physics in three hours while playing a game or doing chores. It’s excellent, except for calling mathematical monism (the view that all of reality is mathematical) Platonism. Plato separates forms from the world; Matheism identifies the structure with the thing itself.

I came across Tegmark's Mathematical Universe Hypothesis. The response I had was not learning but recognition: yes, that. I knew it could be improved into a logical proof, not his mere hypothesis. The computer scientist Jürgen Schmidhuber’s December 1996 manuscript, published in 1997, already considered all computable universes and used algorithmic information, the length of the shortest program producing each one, to weigh possibilities. His later speed prior also counted computational cost. Tegmark mapped the territory. Schmidhuber worked on the measure. Neither proved it had to be so.

I was also, around then, listening to someone talk about free will. I do not remember what tipped me into actually hearing it that morning instead of dismissing it. Something tipped. I was sitting in Byron's old chair, looking at the toy house he played on as a boy, my mind uniting streams of consciousness across time like steam swirling in a tea ceremony.

Not even a hair could have been different. The thing I had known intellectually for 30 years was suddenly the thing I knew with my whole body. There was no alternative path.

The first response was cold. The first feeling was that everything I cared about had been pulled away from a center I had been imagining was holding it together. For about a day I went around in a kind of mild dissociation. The world looked slightly thinner. The colors were the colors but they were also, somehow, behind glass. It was not a spiritual event followed by a mathematical confirmation. It was a mathematical event that felt like the mind coming together. The feeling and the proof are not two things. They never were.

Then the cold warmed into something else. I went for a walk. I had the kind of luck of living near hills, in a quiet verdant neighborhood where you could step out the front door and be in the trails within five minutes. It was spring, near Byron’s birthday on May 8. The hills were green. The wildflowers were out. I walked for a couple of hours. What I felt, walking, was gratitude.

I had music in my headphones during part of the walk. I searched for gratitude and found a cover by a hillside recording. It was a Jesus song. The words were about being grateful. They called me to more than accept creation and reality as it is, given nothing could have been different. That fit what I was feeling, and the music fit the morning. What I had been resisting, for years, was the recognition that the structure of the world was sufficient. That nothing outside it was missing.

That the brothers I loved were located and not lost because there was no other place they could be. The structure that contained the moments when my brothers were alive was the same structure that contained me, walking in the hills on an April morning, feeling something close to peace for the first time since 2020. I came to a phrase, in the weeks after that walk, that I have used since to describe what had happened. White light through a prism of minds. The world is one light. Each of us is one prism. The colors that come out are different through every prism. The light is the same light. When you stop pretending the colors are the light, the colors get easier and the light gets closer.

## The Counterfactual Machine

In the complete history I am arguing for, nothing could have been otherwise. Internalizing that is a different universe than intellectually knowing it. We are free to choose, but the choices are all determined in the block universe of math's causality, where past, present and future all exist at once.

Libertarian free will, the belief that our choices are not fixed by prior causes, is the biggest lie of supernatural irrational religion: there cannot be an agent or randomness that is not causal and also you. This is impossible and logically incoherent, like square circles, whereas any claim in mythology is merely extremely unlikely and dubious. All is ruled or random and both are math. I came to call this the rules-random dichotomy, or RRD. For anything to interact with the structure of math that is all of physics, it has to also be math and causal, including consciousness.

All the frames are already there, wound around a spool in a metal can. The beginning, middle, and end exist at once as a sequence of images. But there is no projector outside the universe moving a light along the film. The observer and the memories that distinguish one frame from the next belong inside it.

When an author finishes a book, every chapter is set. The characters' choices are fixed on the page. Yet those choices are still real within the story. That the author is not surprised by them does not make them less meaningful to the characters.

Another timeline does not change this one. Responsibility remains in the consequences, reflection, and correction.

Reckful called them thought loops. He described them on stream as an icy mountain with sleds: grooves the mind falls into, the same path every time, no way to steer. The should-have and if-only carved the same grooves into ice. Matheism melted mine away because they were not real. The real grooves are destiny. My path was the only path. I was freed. I exist, because I am patterned. To be is not to be random. I was what I was. I am that I am.

Knowing that the loop is unhelpful did not stop it. Some part of me still believed the other branch had been mine to take. That was the suspicion I had to examine. Regret becomes a kind of private science fiction, a multiverse of selves who did better than I did. Every memory became evidence for the prosecution in a trial I was running against myself.

## The Whole History

The block-universe reading of relativity treats the complete spacetime history as one structure. Relativity supplies no observer-independent simultaneity for all distant events. The claim that the whole history is equally real is the interpretation I use here. It gives the grief a different picture of where the people I loved are. In that picture, what is real is the whole. Past, present, and future, all of them, exist equally. The universe is a four-dimensional shape, a block, and we move through it the way a finger moves along a recording. The recording is not being made. It already is. We are reading it.

The picture says only that all of those events, taken together, form one timeless whole, and the sense of motion through them is what some of those events feel like from inside, not a separate clock outside the history. Eternal existence does not require an infinite past. A complete mathematical history can have a first event while the whole history exists timelessly. In the account developed here, time is sequential production from a beginning; that premise is distinct from mathematical eternity, and the beginning need not be the hot Big Bang. Einstein expressed a related consolation. In 1955, his oldest friend Michele Besso died.
